-2(\partial_{t}A^{li})(\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}\partial_{l}f)-6(\mathfrak{D}A^{li})(\mathfrak{D}^{5}\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{l}f)
which comes from
[\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2},A^{li},\partial_{l}f]
. By mimicing the treatment of
C_{2}(Q)
and
C_{2}(v)
in (
5.5
)-(
5.6
), we can define the modified Alinhac good unknowns in the case of
\partial_{*}^{I}=\partial_{t}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-N}~(2\leq N\leq 7)
as the following

\mathbf{V}^{*}=\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2}v-\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v+\Delta_{v}^{*},~~\mathbf{Q}^{*}=\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2}Q-\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}Q,

(5.63)

where

(\Delta_{v}^{*})_{i}:=-6\mathfrak{D}^{5}\partial_{t}^{2}v\cdot\nabla_{A}\mathfrak{D}\eta_{i}-2\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}v\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i}

(5.64)

and

\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2}(\text{div}_{A}v)=\nabla_{A}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{*}+C^{*}(v),~~~\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2}(\nabla_{A}Q)=\nabla_{A}\mathbf{Q}^{*}+C^{*}(Q),

(5.65)

with

\|C^{*}(f)\|_{0}\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}(t))\|f\|_{8,*}.

In this case,
\Delta_{Q}^{*}=0
, and thus the boundary integrals
IB_{3}^{*},IB_{4}^{*},IB_{5}^{*}
all vanish. The analogues of
IB_{1}^{*},IB_{2}^{*},IB_{6}^{*}
in this case can still be controlled in the same way as in Section
5.2
. In the control of the difference between
\mathbf{V}^{*}
and
\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2}
, we have by (
5.63
)-(
5.64
) that

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{V}^{*}\|_{0}+\|\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}v\|_{0}\|\nabla_{A}v\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathfrak{D}^{5}\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0}\|\nabla_{A}\mathfrak{D}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}

(5.66)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{V}^{*}\|_{0}+\|\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}v\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla_{A}v\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathfrak{D}^{5}\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla_{A}\mathfrak{D}\eta\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{V}^{*}\|_{0}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P\left(\|\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0},\|\mathfrak{D}^{5}\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0},\|\partial\mathfrak{D}v\|_{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\|\mathbf{V}^{*}\|_{0}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t

The remaining analysis should follow in the same way as in Section
5.2
and
5.3
so we omit the details. The result is

\|\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\|\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2}q\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}\left|A^{3i}\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}\bigg|_{t=T}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t,

(5.67)

where
\mathfrak{D}^{6}
contains at least one spatial derivative
\overline{\partial}{}
.

5.5
The case of full time derivatives

In the case of full time derivatives, the modified Alinhac good unknown is still defined similarly as in (
5.63
)-(
5.65
):

\mathbf{V}^{*}=\partial_{t}^{8}v-\partial_{t}^{8}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v+\Delta_{v}^{*},~~\mathbf{Q}^{*}=\partial_{t}^{8}Q-\partial_{t}^{8}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}Q,

(5.68)

where

(\Delta_{v}^{*})_{i}:=-8\partial_{t}^{7}v\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i}

(5.69)

and

\partial_{t}^{8}(\text{div}_{A}v)=\nabla_{A}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{*}+C^{*}(v),~~~\partial_{t}^{8}(\nabla_{A}Q)=\nabla_{A}\mathbf{Q}^{*}+C^{*}(Q),

(5.70)

with

\|C^{*}(f)\|_{0}\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}(t))\|f\|_{8,*}.

Extra difficulty: trace lemma is no longer applicable

When
\partial_{*}^{I}=\partial_{t}^{8}
, there are terms of the form
\partial_{t}^{7}v
in the boundary integrals. In the case of full time derivative, one cannot apply Lemma
3.1
to control
|\partial_{t}^{7}v|_{0}
. This difficulty appears in the estimates of the analogue of
IB_{6}^{*}
.
Instead, we need to write
IB_{6}^{*}
in terms of interior integrals by using a similar technique in (
4.42
).

\displaystyle IB_{6}^{*}=

\displaystyle-8\int_{\Gamma}\hat{A}^{3i}N_{3}~\partial_{t}^{8}\eta_{p}~A^{3p}\partial_{3}Q~\partial_{t}^{7}v_{r}~A^{lr}\partial_{l}v_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}=-8\int_{\Gamma}\hat{A}^{3i}N_{3}~\partial_{t}^{7}v_{p}~A^{3p}\partial_{3}Q~\partial_{t}^{7}v_{r}~A^{lr}\partial_{l}v_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

(5.71)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-8\int_{\Omega}\hat{A}^{3i}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v_{p}~A^{3p}\partial_{3}Q~\partial_{t}^{7}v_{r}~A^{lr}\partial_{l}v_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y-8\int_{\Omega}\hat{A}^{3i}\partial_{t}^{7}v_{p}~A^{3p}\partial_{3}Q~\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v_{r}~A^{lr}\partial_{l}v_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle-8\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{7}v_{p}~\partial_{t}^{7}v_{r}~\partial_{3}(\hat{A}^{3i}A^{3p}\partial_{3}Q~A^{lr}\partial_{l}v_{i})\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle IB_{61}^{*}+IB_{62}^{*}+IB_{63}^{*}.

The term
IB_{63}^{*}
can be directly controlled

IB_{63}^{*}\lesssim P(\|\partial_{t}^{7}v\|_{0},\|\partial v\|_{3},\|\partial Q\|_{3},\|A\|_{3})\lesssim P(\|v\|_{8,*},\|Q\|_{8,*},\|\eta\|_{4}).

(5.72)

The term
IB_{61}^{*},IB_{62}^{*}
should be controlled by integrating
\partial_{t}
by parts under time integral.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}IB_{61}^{*}=

\displaystyle-8\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\hat{A}^{3i}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v_{p}~A^{3p}\partial_{3}Q~\partial_{t}^{7}v_{r}~A^{lr}\partial_{l}v_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}t

(5.73)

\displaystyle\overset{\partial_{t}}{=}

\displaystyle-8\int_{\Omega}\hat{A}^{3i}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{6}v_{p}~A^{3p}\partial_{3}Q~\partial_{t}^{7}v_{r}~A^{lr}\partial_{l}v_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle+8\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\hat{A}^{3i}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{6}v_{p}~A^{3p}\partial_{3}Q~\partial_{t}^{8}v_{r}~A^{lr}\partial_{l}v_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+8\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\hat{A}^{3i}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{6}v_{p}~\partial_{t}^{7}v_{r}~\partial_{t}(A^{3p}\partial_{3}Q~A^{lr}\partial_{l}v_{i})\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{6}v\|_{0}\|\partial_{t}^{7}v\|_{0}P(\|A\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial Q\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{6}v\|_{0}\left(\|\partial_{t}^{8}v\|_{0}P(\|A\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial Q\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}})+\|\partial_{t}^{7}v\|_{0}\|A\cdot\partial_{t}(A\cdot\partial Q\cdot A\cdot\partial v)\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{6}v\|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P\left(\|\partial_{t}^{8}v\|_{0},\|(\partial_{t}A,\partial_{t}\partial Q,\partial_{t}\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\right)+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|v\|_{8,*},\|Q\|_{7,*},\|\eta\|_{3})\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{6}v\|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t,

IB_{62}^{*}
can be controlled in the same way, so we omit the details. Summarizing the estimates above, we get the energy inequality of the full time derivatives

\|\partial_{t}^{8}v\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{8}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{8}q\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}\left|A^{3i}\partial_{t}^{8}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}\bigg|_{t=T}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{6}v\|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t,

(5.74)

which together with (
5.54
), (
5.61
), (
5.67
) concludes the proof of Proposition
5.1
.

6
Control of mixed non-weighted derivatives

The case of mixed non-weighted derivatives correspond to
\partial_{*}^{I}=\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}
with
1\leq i_{3}\leq 3,~i_{4}=0
. In this case, the modified Alinhac good unknowns introduced in Section
5
are still needed when commuting
\partial_{*}^{I}
with
\nabla_{A}
. On the other hand, the highest order term
\partial_{*}^{I}Q
no longer vanishes on the boundary due to the presence of normal derivatives, so we need to use the method in Section
4
to deal with the boundary integral. Therefore, we should combine the methods in Section
4
and Section
5
to get the control of mixed non-weighted derivatives. The result of this section is

Proposition 6.1
.

The following energy inequality holds for sufficiently small
\varepsilon>0

\sum_{1\leq i_{3}\leq 3,~i_{4}=0}\|\partial_{*}^{I}v\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{*}^{I}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{*}^{I}q\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}\left|A^{3i}\partial_{*}^{I}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}\bigg|_{t=T}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\partial_{3}^{4}v\|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(6.1)

6.1
Purely spatial derivatives

We still start with the control of purely spatial derivatives. Let
N=1,2,3
and we consider
\partial_{*}^{I}=\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}
.

6.1.1
The modified Alinhac good unknowns

Similarly as in Section
5.1.1
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}(\nabla_{A}^{i}f)=

\displaystyle\nabla_{A}^{i}(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}f)+(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}A^{li})\partial_{l}f+[\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N},A^{li},\partial_{l}f]

(6.2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{A}^{i}(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}f)-\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}(A^{lr}~\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{m}\eta_{r}~A^{mi})\partial_{l}f+[\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N},A^{li},\partial_{l}f]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{A}^{i}(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}f-\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{r}~A^{lr}~\partial_{l}f)+(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{r})\nabla_{A}^{i}(\nabla_{A}^{r}f)

\displaystyle-([\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N},A^{lr}A^{mi}]\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{m}\eta_{r})\partial_{l}f+[\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N},A^{li},\partial_{l}f],

where the last line still contains the terms whose
L^{2}(\Omega)
-norms cannot be directly bounded under the setting of anisotropic Sobolev space
H_{*}^{8}(\Omega)
. The reason is that
\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}
may fall on
A=\partial\eta\times\partial\eta
and
\partial_{l}f
. The following quantities are exactly these terms.

\displaystyle e_{1}^{\sharp}:=-\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}(A^{lr}A^{mi})(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{m}\eta_{r})\partial_{l}f,

\displaystyle e_{2}^{\sharp}:=-(7-2N)\overline{\partial}{}(A^{lr}A^{mi})(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\partial_{m}\eta_{r})\partial_{l}f,

(6.3)

\displaystyle e_{3}^{\sharp}:=(8-2N)(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}A^{li})(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{l}f),

\displaystyle e_{4}^{\sharp}:=(8-2N)(\overline{\partial}{}A^{li})(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\partial_{l}f).

One can mimic the derivation of (
5.18
) and (
5.19
) to define the “modified Alinhac good unknowns” of
v
and
Q
with respect to
\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}
to be

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{i}^{\sharp}:=

\displaystyle\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}v_{i}-\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i}

(6.4)

\displaystyle-(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}v_{i}-(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}v\cdot\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}\eta_{i}

\displaystyle+(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i}+(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v\cdot\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}\eta_{i},

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp}:=

\displaystyle\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}Q-\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}Q

(6.5)

\displaystyle-(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}Q+(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}Q.

Then
\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}
and
\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp}
satisfy the following relations

\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}(\text{div}_{A}v)=\nabla_{A}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}+C^{\sharp}(v),~~~\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}(\nabla_{A}Q)=\nabla_{A}\mathbf{Q}^{*}+C^{\sharp}(Q),

(6.6)

where the commutator
C^{\sharp}
satisfies

\|C^{\sharp}(f)\|_{0}\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}(t))\|f\|_{8,*}.

(6.7)

Denote
\Delta_{v}^{\sharp}
and
\Delta_{Q}^{\sharp}
to be

\displaystyle(\Delta_{v}^{\sharp})_{i}:=

\displaystyle-(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}v_{i}-(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}v\cdot\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}\eta_{i}

\displaystyle+(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i}+(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v\cdot\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}\eta_{i},

\displaystyle\Delta_{Q}^{\sharp}:=

\displaystyle-(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}Q+(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}Q.

Then we can derive the evolution equation of
\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}
and
\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp}

\displaystyle R\partial_{t}\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}-J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)+\nabla_{A}\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp}

(6.8)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[R,\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}]\partial_{t}v+\left[J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial),\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\right]\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)

\displaystyle+C^{\sharp}(Q)+R\partial_{t}(-\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v+\Delta_{v}^{\sharp}).

Denote the RHS of (
6.8
) to be
\mathbf{F}^{\sharp}
, then direct computation yields that

\|\mathbf{F}^{\sharp}\|_{0}\lesssim P\left(\|\eta\|_{8,*},\|v\|_{8,*},\|Q\|_{8,*}\right).

Now we take
L^{2}(\Omega)
inner product of (
6.8
) and
J\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}
to get the following energy identity

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\rho_{0}|\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y=\int_{\Omega}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}-\int_{\Omega}(\nabla_{\hat{A}}Q)\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}+\int_{\Omega}J\mathbf{F}^{\sharp}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}.

(6.9)

6.1.2
Interior estimates

The last integral on RHS of (
6.9
) is directly controlled

\int_{\Omega}J\mathbf{F}^{\sharp}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}\lesssim\int_{\Omega}\|\mathbf{F}^{\sharp}\|_{0}\|\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}\|_{0}.

(6.10)

Then for the first term on RHS of (
6.9
) we integrate
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
by parts to produce the energy of magnetic field. Again, there is one term which cannot be directly controlled but will cancel with another term produced by
-\int_{\Omega}(\nabla_{\hat{A}}Q)\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}
. The proof follows in the same way as (
4.13
) so we omit the details.

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}\,\mathrm{d}y

(6.11)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}J\left|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y+K_{11}^{\sharp}+P\left(\left\|(\eta,v,b_{0},(b_{0}\cdot\partial))\right\|_{8,*}\right),

where

K_{11}^{\sharp}:=-\int_{\Omega}J\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\cdot\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}(\text{div}_{A}v)\,\mathrm{d}y.

(6.12)

Next we analyze the term
-\int_{\Omega}(\nabla_{\hat{A}}Q)\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}
. Integrating by parts and using Piola’s identity
\partial_{l}\hat{A}^{li}=0
, we get

-\int_{\Omega}(\nabla_{\hat{A}}Q)\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}\,\mathrm{d}y=\int_{\Omega}J\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp}(\nabla_{A}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sharp})\,\mathrm{d}y-\int_{\Gamma}J\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp}A^{li}N_{l}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{\sharp}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}=:I^{\sharp}+IB^{\sharp}.

(6.13)

Plugging (
6.6
) into
I^{\sharp}
, we get

\displaystyle I^{\sharp}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}J\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}q\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}(\text{div}_{A}v)+\int_{\Omega}J\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left|J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right|^{2}\right)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}(\text{div}_{A}v)

(6.14)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\left(-(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{p})\hat{A}^{lp}~\partial_{l}Q+\Delta_{Q}^{\sharp}\right)~\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}(\text{div}_{A}v)-\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}Q)C^{\sharp}(v)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle I_{1}^{\sharp}+I_{2}^{\sharp}+I_{3}^{\sharp}+I_{4}^{\sharp},

where
I_{4}^{\sharp}
can be directly controlled by using the estimates of
C^{\sharp}(v)

I_{4}^{\sharp}\lesssim\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}Q\|_{0}\|C^{\sharp}(v)\|_{0}\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{8,*})\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}Q\|_{0}\|v\|_{8,*}.

(6.15)

The term
I_{1}^{\sharp}
produces the energy of fluid pressure

I_{1}^{\sharp}\lesssim-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{J^{2}R^{\prime}(q)}{\rho_{0}}\left|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}q\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y+P(\|q\|_{8,*},\|\rho_{0}\|_{8,*},\|\eta\|_{8,*}).

(6.16)

Similarly as in (
5.33
), the term
I_{2}^{\sharp}
produces the cancellation with
K_{11}^{\sharp}
.

\displaystyle I_{2}^{\sharp}=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{\Omega}J\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\cdot\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}(\text{div}_{A}v)}_{\text{exactly cancel with }K_{11}^{\sharp}}\,\mathrm{d}y

(6.17)

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq N_{1}+N_{2}=8}\binom{N}{N_{1}}\binom{8-2N}{N_{2}}\int_{\Omega}J\partial_{3}^{N_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}^{N_{2}}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\cdot\partial_{3}^{N-N_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N-N_{2}}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}(\text{div}_{A}v)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-K_{11}^{\sharp}

\displaystyle-\sum_{1\leq N_{1}+N_{2}=8}\binom{N}{N_{1}}\binom{8-2N}{N_{2}}\int_{\Omega}\frac{J^{2}R^{\prime}(q)}{\rho_{0}}\partial_{3}^{N_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}^{N_{2}}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\cdot\partial_{3}^{N-N_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N-N_{2}}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\partial_{t}q)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle-\sum_{1\leq N_{1}+N_{2}=8}\binom{N}{N_{1}}\binom{8-2N}{N_{2}}\int_{\Omega}J\partial_{3}^{N_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}^{N_{2}}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\cdot\partial_{3}^{N-N_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N-N_{2}}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\left(\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{8},\frac{JR^{\prime}(q)}{\rho_{0}}\right]\partial_{t}q\right)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle-K_{11}^{\sharp}+I_{21}^{\sharp}+I_{22}^{\sharp}

and by direct computation we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}I_{21}^{\sharp}\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}q\|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t

(6.18)

\displaystyle I_{22}^{\sharp}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{7,*}^{2}\|q\|_{8,*}.

(6.19)

Then
I_{3}^{\sharp}
can be controlled by integrating
\partial_{t}
by parts under time integral after invoking
\text{div}_{A}v=-\frac{JR^{\prime}(q)}{\rho_{0}}\partial_{t}q
. The proof is similar to (
5.37
) so we do not repeat the proof.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}I_{3}^{\sharp}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\partial_{3}^{N}q\|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(6.20)

Summarizing (
6.14
)-(
6.20
) and choosing
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, we get the interior estimates

\int_{0}^{T}I^{\sharp}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\frac{J^{2}R^{\prime}(q)}{\rho_{0}}\left|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}q\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{T}_{0}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(6.21)

Therefore, it suffices to analyze the boundary integral
IB^{\sharp}
.

6.1.3
Boundary estimates

Invoking (
6.4
)-(
6.5
), the boundary integral now reads

\displaystyle IB^{\sharp}=-\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp}JA^{3i}N_{3}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{\sharp}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}JA^{3i}N_{3}(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}Q)\mathbf{V}_{i}^{\sharp}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

(6.22)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Gamma}\hat{A}^{3i}N_{3}(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{p})A^{3p}\partial_{3}Q~\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}v_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}\hat{A}^{3i}N_{3}(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{p}~A^{3p}\partial_{3}Q)(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i})\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}\hat{A}^{3i}N_{3}(\Delta_{Q}^{\sharp})(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}v_{i})\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}+\int_{\Gamma}\hat{A}^{3i}N_{3}(\Delta_{Q}^{\sharp})\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}\hat{A}^{3i}N_{3}\Delta_{Q}^{\sharp}(\Delta_{v}^{\sharp})_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}+\int_{\Gamma}\hat{A}^{3i}N_{3}(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{p}~A^{3p}~\partial_{3}Q)(\Delta_{v}^{\sharp})_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle IB_{0}^{\sharp}+IB_{1}^{\sharp}+IB_{2}^{\sharp}+IB_{3}^{\sharp}+IB_{4}^{\sharp}+IB_{5}^{\sharp}+IB_{6}^{\sharp}.

To control
IB^{\sharp}
, we only need to combine the techniques used in Section
4.3
and Section
5.2.2
:

•

IB_{1}^{\sharp},IB_{2}^{\sharp}
together with the Rayleigh-Taylor sign condition produces the boundary energy
|A^{3i}\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{i}|_{0}^{2}
, similarly as
IB_{1}+IB_{2}
in Section
4.3
and
IB_{1}^{*}+IB_{2}^{*}
Section
5.2.2
.

•

The term
IB_{0}^{\sharp}
is the analogue of
IB_{0}
in Section
4.3
and can be controlled with similar method as in Section
4.3
.

•

IB_{3}^{\sharp}\sim IB_{6}^{\sharp}
are the analogues of
IB_{3}^{*}\sim IB_{6}^{*}
in Section
5.2.2
. These terms can be controlled exactly in the same way as
IB_{3}^{*}\sim IB_{6}^{*}
.

First,
IB_{1}^{\sharp}
and
IB_{2}^{\sharp}
give the boundary energy with the help of Rayleigh-Taylor sign condition. The proof is exactly the same as in Section
4.3
and Section
5.2.2
by merely replacing
\partial_{3}^{4}
or
\overline{\partial}{}^{8}
with
\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}
, so we do not repeat the computations here.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}IB_{1}^{\sharp}+IB_{2}^{\sharp}=

\displaystyle-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\left|A^{3i}\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}\bigg|^{T}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(6.23)

We then analyze
IB_{0}^{\sharp}
. Invoking (
6.4
), we have

\displaystyle IB_{0}^{\sharp}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}N_{3}(J\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}Q)(A^{3i}~\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}v_{i})\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}+\int_{\Gamma}JA^{3i}N_{3}(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}Q)(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i})\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

(6.24)

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}JA^{3i}N_{3}(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}Q)(\Delta_{v}^{\sharp})_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle IB_{01}^{\sharp}+IB_{02}^{\sharp}+IB_{03}^{\sharp}.

We note that
IB_{01}^{\sharp}
and
IB_{02}^{\sharp}
are the analogues of
IB_{01}
and
IB_{02}
in Section
4.3
, so we do not repeat all the details here. The extra term
IB_{03}^{\sharp}
can be directly controlled (cf. (
6.31
) below).

We differentiate the continuity equation (
4.29
) by
\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}
to simplify the top order term containing
v
in
IB_{01}^{\sharp}
:

\displaystyle A^{3i}\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}v_{i}=

\displaystyle-\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\left(\frac{JR^{\prime}(q)}{\rho_{0}}\partial_{t}q\right)-\sum_{L=1}^{2}\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}(A^{Li}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i})

(6.25)

\displaystyle-\sum_{N_{1}+N_{2}\geq 1,N_{1}\leq N-1}\binom{N-1}{N_{1}}\binom{8-2N}{N_{2}}\left(\partial_{3}^{N_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}^{N_{2}}A^{3i}\right)\left(\partial_{3}^{N-N_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N-N_{2}}v_{i}\right),

where the term contains
\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}A^{Li}=\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta\times\overline{\partial}{}\eta+
controllable terms, where
\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta
cannot be controlled on the boundary. Invoking (
3.3
) with
D=\overline{\partial}{}
, we expand this problematic term to be

\displaystyle(\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}A^{Li})\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i}=

\displaystyle-\left(\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}(A^{Lp}~\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{m}\eta_{p}~A^{mi})\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i}

(6.26)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-A^{Lp}~\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{p}~A^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i}-\sum_{M=1}^{2}A^{Lp}(\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\overline{\partial}{}_{M}\eta_{p})A^{Mi}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i}-([\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N},A^{Lp}A^{mi}]\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{m}\eta_{p})\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i}.

On the other hand, in
IB_{02}^{\sharp}
we have

\displaystyle A^{3i}\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i}=A^{3i}\sum_{L=1}^{2}\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{p}A^{Lp}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i}+A^{3i}\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{p}A^{3p}\partial_{3}v_{i},

(6.27)

where the first term exactly cancels with the first term in the RHS of (
6.26
). In fact, this is the analogue of (
4.33
)-(
4.36
) by merely replacing
\partial_{3}^{4}
with
\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}
. Thus we get the cancellation of the top order terms in
IB_{01}^{\sharp}
and
IB_{02}^{\sharp}
.

The second term in (
6.27
) could be treated similarly as in (
4.40
) by invoking
A^{3p}\partial_{3}\eta_{p}=1

\displaystyle\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{p}A^{3p}=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}(\partial_{3}\eta_{p}A^{3p})}_{=0}-(\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}A^{3p})\partial_{3}\eta_{p}-(\overline{\partial}{}A^{3p})(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta_{p})+\text{lower order terms}.

(6.28)

To control
IB_{0}^{\sharp}
, we still need to analyze
\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}Q
. Following the aruments in (
4.37
)-(
4.39
) and replacing
\partial_{3}^{4}
with
\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}
, we can reduce one normal derivative of
Q
to one tangential derivative of
v
and
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
via

\displaystyle\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}Q=

\displaystyle J^{-1}\partial_{3}\eta_{i}\left(\rho_{0}\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\partial_{t}v^{i}+(b_{0}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{i})\right)-\sum_{L=1}^{2}\hat{A}^{Li}(\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}Q)

(6.29)

\displaystyle-(N-1)(\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\hat{A}^{3i})(\partial_{3}Q)+\text{ lower order terms. }

Plugging the expression of
\Delta_{v}^{\sharp}
and (
6.25
)-(
6.29
) into (
6.24
), we find that every highest order term in
IB_{0}^{\sharp}
must be one of the following forms

\displaystyle K_{1}^{\sharp}:=

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}N_{3}(\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\mathfrak{D}f)(\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{9-2N}g)(\partial h)r\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime},

\displaystyle K_{2}^{\sharp}:=

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}N_{3}(\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\mathfrak{D}f)(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}g)(\partial\overline{\partial}{}h)r\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime},

\displaystyle K_{3}^{\sharp}:=

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}N_{3}(\partial_{3}^{N-1}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\mathfrak{D}f)(\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}g)(\partial h)r\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime},

where
\mathfrak{D}=\overline{\partial}{}
or
\partial_{t}
or
(b_{0}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})
, the functions
f,g,h
can be
\eta,v,Q,J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
, and
r
contains at most one derivative of
\eta,~v
or
Q
. We note that the term
K_{2}^{\sharp}
comes from
IB_{03}^{\sharp}
where
\Delta_{v}^{\sharp}
contributes to
\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}g\cdot\partial\overline{\partial}{}h\cdot r
.

Since
1\leq N\leq 3
, we know
7-2N\geq 1
and thus we can directly apply lemma
3.1
to control
K_{1}^{\sharp}\sim K_{3}^{\sharp}
.

\displaystyle K_{1}^{\sharp}\lesssim

\displaystyle|\partial_{3}^{N-1}\mathfrak{D}f|_{8-2N}|\partial_{3}^{N-1}g|_{9-2N}|\partial h~r|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\partial_{3}^{N-1}\mathfrak{D}f\|_{H_{*}^{9-2N}}\|\partial_{3}^{N-1}g\|_{H_{*}^{10-2N}}\|\partial h~r\|_{H^{2}}

(6.30)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|f\|_{2(N-1)+1+(9-2N),*}\|g\|_{2(N-1)+(10-2N),*}\|h\|_{3}\|r\|_{2}=\|f\|_{8,*}\|g\|_{8,*}\|h\|_{3}\|r\|_{2}.

and

\displaystyle K_{2}^{\sharp}\lesssim

\displaystyle|\partial_{3}^{N-1}\mathfrak{D}f|_{8-2N}|\partial_{3}^{N}g|_{7-2N}|(\partial\overline{\partial}{}h)r|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim|\partial_{3}^{N-1}\mathfrak{D}f|_{8-2N}|\partial_{3}^{N}g|_{7-2N}|(\partial\overline{\partial}{}h)r|_{1.5}

(6.31)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial_{3}^{N-1}\mathfrak{D}f\|_{H_{*}^{9-2N}}\|\partial_{3}^{N}g\|_{H_{*}^{8-2N}}\|(\partial\overline{\partial}{}h)r\|_{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{8,*}\|g\|_{8,*}\|h\|_{7,*}\|r\|_{2},

and

\displaystyle K_{3}^{\sharp}\lesssim

\displaystyle|\partial_{3}^{N-1}\mathfrak{D}f|_{8-2N}|\partial_{3}^{N}g|_{7-2N}|(\partial h)r|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim|\partial_{3}^{N-1}\mathfrak{D}f|_{8-2N}|\partial_{3}^{N}g|_{7-2N}|(\partial h)r|_{1.5}

(6.32)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial_{3}^{N-1}\mathfrak{D}f\|_{H_{*}^{9-2N}}\|\partial_{3}^{N}g\|_{H_{*}^{8-2N}}\|(\partial h)r\|_{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{8,*}\|g\|_{8,*}\|h\|_{3}\|r\|_{2}.

One can use either trace lemma or similar techniques as in (
4.41
)-(
4.43
) to analyze the remaining terms which are all of lower order than
K_{1}^{\sharp}\sim K_{3}^{\sharp}
. This completes the control of
IB_{0}^{\sharp}
.

The analysis of
IB_{3}^{\sharp}\sim IB_{6}^{\sharp}
can be proceeded exactly in the same way as
IB_{3}^{*}\sim IB_{6}^{*}
. Since these quantities involving the modification terms
\Delta_{Q}^{\sharp},\Delta_{v}^{\sharp}
are of lower order, we do not repeat the details again. We can finally prove that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}IB_{3}^{\sharp}+IB_{4}^{\sharp}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left|A^{3i}\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}P\left(\|(\eta,v,b)\|_{8,*},\|Q\|_{8,*},\|\rho_{0}\|_{3}\right)\,\mathrm{d}t,

(6.33)

\displaystyle+\left|A^{3i}\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}P(\|\eta\|_{8,*},\|Q\|_{8,*})\int_{0}^{T}\|v(t)\|_{8,*}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle IB_{5}^{\sharp}\lesssim

\displaystyle|\hat{A}^{3i}|_{L^{\infty}}|\Delta_{Q}^{\sharp}|_{0}|(\Delta_{v}^{\sharp})_{i}|_{0}\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{8,*},\|v\|_{8,*},\|Q\|_{7,*}),

(6.34)

\displaystyle IB_{6}^{\sharp}\lesssim

\displaystyle|\hat{A}^{3i}\partial_{3}Q|_{L^{\infty}}|A^{3p}\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{p}|_{0}|(\Delta_{v}^{\sharp})_{i}|_{0}\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{8,*},\|v\|_{8,*},\|Q\|_{7,*}).

(6.35)

Summarizing the estimates above, we get the control of the boundary integral

\int_{0}^{T}IB^{\sharp}\lesssim-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\left|A^{3i}\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(6.36)

Combining (
6.9
)-(
6.11
), (
6.21
), (
6.36
) and choosing
\varepsilon>0
in (
6.18
) to be suitably small, we get the following inequality

\|\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}q\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}\left|A^{3i}\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}\bigg|_{t=T}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(6.37)

Finally, invoking (
6.4
), we get the
\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}~(N=1,2,3)
-estimates of
v

\displaystyle\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}v\|_{0}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}\|_{0}+\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta\|_{0}\|\nabla_{A}v_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta\|_{0}\left(\|\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}v\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)+\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}v\|_{0}\|\nabla_{A}\overline{\partial}{}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Since
\partial^{m}\eta|_{t=0}=0
for any
m\geq 2,m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we know

\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}v\|_{0}\lesssim\|\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}\|_{0}+P(\|v\|_{7,*},\|\eta\|_{7,*})\int_{0}^{T}P(\|v\|_{8,*}),

(6.38)

and thus

\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}v\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}q\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}\left|A^{3i}\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}\bigg|_{t=T}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(6.39)

6.2
Control of time derivatives

In the case of
\partial_{*}^{I}=\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{8-2N-k}\partial_{t}^{k}
for
1\leq k\leq 8-2N
, most of steps in the proof are still applicable. However, the presence of time derivative(s) could simplify the “modified Alinhac good unknowns”. We note that most of the modifications are essentially similar to Section (
5.3
)
\sim
Section
5.5
, so we no longer repeat the details.

6.2.1
One time derivative

When
k=1
, the modified Alinhac good unknowns can be defined by replacing
8\overline{\partial}{}^{7}
by
(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}
in Section
5.3.2
, i.e.,

\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}=\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\partial_{t}v-\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\partial_{t}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v+\Delta_{v}^{\sharp},~~\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp}=\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\partial_{t}Q-\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\partial_{t}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}Q+\Delta_{Q}^{\sharp}.,

(6.40)

where

\displaystyle(\Delta_{v}^{\sharp})_{i}:=

\displaystyle-(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}\partial_{t}v_{i}-(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}v\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i}+(16-4N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\cdot\nabla_{A}v\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i},

(6.41)

\displaystyle\Delta_{Q}^{\sharp}:=

\displaystyle-(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}\partial_{t}Q+(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v\cdot\nabla_{A}Q,

and

\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\partial_{t}(\text{div}_{A}v)=\nabla_{A}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}+C^{\sharp}(v),~~~\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\partial_{t}(\nabla_{A}Q)=\nabla_{A}\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp}+C^{\sharp}(Q),

(6.42)

with

\|C^{\sharp}(f)\|_{0}\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}(t))\|f\|_{8,*}.

The difference between
\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}v
and
\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}
should be controlled in the same way as (
5.60
) by replacing
\overline{\partial}{}^{7}
with
\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}

\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\partial_{t}v\|_{0}\lesssim\|\mathbf{V}^{*}\|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t,

(6.43)

and thus

\displaystyle\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\partial_{t}v\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\partial_{t}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\partial_{t}q\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}\left|A^{3i}\partial_{3}^{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{7-2N}\partial_{t}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}\bigg|_{t=T}

(6.44)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

6.2.2
2
\sim
(7-2N) time derivatives

When
2\leq k\leq 7-2N
, we can mimic the analysis in Section
5.4
: We just need to replace
\mathfrak{D}^{6}\partial_{t}^{2}
by
\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}\partial_{t}^{2}
where
\mathfrak{D}
denotes
\overline{\partial}{}
or
\partial_{t}
and
\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}
contains at least one
\overline{\partial}{}
. The analogous problematic term becomes
-2(\partial_{t}A^{li})(\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}\partial_{t}\partial_{l}f)-(6-2N)(\mathfrak{D}A^{li})(\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{5-2N}\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{l}f)
which comes from
[\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}\partial_{t}^{2},A^{li},\partial_{l}f]
. Following (
5.63
)-(
5.65
), we can similarly define

\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}=\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}\partial_{t}^{2}v-\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v+\Delta_{v}^{\sharp},~~\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp}=\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}\partial_{t}^{2}Q-\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}Q,

(6.45)

where

(\Delta_{v}^{\sharp})_{i}:=-(6-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{5-2N}\partial_{t}^{2}v\cdot\nabla_{A}\mathfrak{D}\eta_{i}-2\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}\partial_{t}v\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i}

(6.46)

and

\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}\partial_{t}^{2}(\text{div}_{A}v)=\nabla_{A}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}+C^{\sharp}(v),~~~\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}\partial_{t}^{2}(\nabla_{A}Q)=\nabla_{A}\mathbf{Q}^{*}+C^{\sharp}(Q),

(6.47)

with

\|C^{\sharp}(f)\|_{0}\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}(t))\|f\|_{8,*}.

Again we have
\Delta_{Q}^{\sharp}
in this case, and thus the analogues of
IB_{3}^{\sharp}\sim IB_{5}^{\sharp}
all vanish. The boundary integrals
IB_{0}^{\sharp},IB_{1}^{\sharp},IB_{2}^{\sharp},IB_{6}^{\sharp}
and the interior terms can be controlled in the same way as Section
6.1
. Finally, one has

\displaystyle\|\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}\partial_{t}^{2}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}\partial_{t}^{2}q\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}\left|A^{3i}\partial_{3}^{N}\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}\partial_{t}^{2}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}\bigg|_{t=T}

(6.48)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t,

where
\mathfrak{D}^{6-2N}
contains at least one spatial derivative
\overline{\partial}{}
.

6.2.3
Full time derivatives

When
\partial_{*}^{I}=\partial_{3}^{N}\partial_{t}^{8-2N}
for
N=1,2,3
, there is not tangential spatial derivative on the boundary and thus Lemma
3.1
is no longer applicable. In this case, the modified Alinhac good unknowns become

\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}=\partial_{3}^{N}\partial_{t}^{8-2N}v-\partial_{3}^{N}\partial_{t}^{8-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v+\Delta_{v}^{\sharp},~~\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp}=\partial_{3}^{N}\partial_{t}^{8-2N}Q-\partial_{3}^{N}\partial_{t}^{8-2N}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}Q,

(6.49)

where

(\Delta_{v}^{\sharp})_{i}:=-(8-2N)\partial_{3}^{N}\partial_{t}^{8-2N}v\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i}

(6.50)

and

\partial_{3}^{N}\partial_{t}^{8-2N}(\text{div}_{A}v)=\nabla_{A}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sharp}+C^{\sharp}(v),~~~\partial_{3}^{N}\partial_{t}^{8-2N}(\nabla_{A}Q)=\nabla_{A}\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp}+C^{\sharp}(Q),

(6.51)

with

\|C^{\sharp}(f)\|_{0}\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}(t))\|f\|_{8,*}.

The proof follows in the same way as Section
5.5
after replacing
\partial_{t}^{7}
by
\partial_{t}^{7-2N}
and the coefficient 8 by
(8-2N)
. So we no longer repeat the details. Finally, we can get

\displaystyle\|\partial_{3}^{N}\partial_{t}^{8-2N}v\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{3}^{N}\partial_{t}^{8-2N}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{3}^{N}\partial_{t}^{8-2N}q\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}\left|A^{3i}\partial_{3}^{N}\partial_{t}^{8-2N}\eta_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}\bigg|_{t=T}

(6.52)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\partial_{3}^{N+1}\partial_{t}^{6-2N}v\|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t,

which together with (
6.39
), (
6.44
), (
6.48
) concludes the proof of Proposition
6.1
.

7
Control of weighted normal derivatives

Now we consider the most general case
\partial_{*}^{I}=\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}
with
i_{1}+i_{2}+2i_{3}+i_{4}=8
and
i_{4}>0
. The presence of the weighted normal derivatives
(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}}
makes the following difference from the non-weighted case.

1.

Extra terms are produced when we commute
\partial_{*}^{I}
with
\partial_{3}
because
\sigma
is a function of
y_{3}
. Once
\partial_{3}
falls on the weight function, we will lose a weight and
(\sigma\partial_{3})
becomes
\partial_{3}
, which causes a loss of derivative. This appears when we commute
\partial_{*}^{I}
with
\nabla_{A}^{i}
that falls on
Q
or
v_{i}
and commute
\partial_{*}^{I}
with
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
.

2.

There is no boundary integral because the weight function
\sigma
vanishes on
\Gamma
.

To overcome the difficulty mentioned above, we can again use the techniques, similar with those in the previous sections.

•

Invoke the MHD equation and the continuity equation to replace
\nabla_{A}Q
and
\text{div}_{A}v
by tangential derivatives.

•

Produce a weight funtion by using
b_{0}^{3}|_{\Gamma}=0
and
\overline{\partial}{}Q|_{\Gamma}=0
.

•

In particular, if
\partial_{*}^{I}
does not contain time derivative, we need to add an extra modification term in the good unknown of
v
.

First we analyze
[(b_{0}\cdot\partial),\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}]f
. Compared with the non-weighted case, we need to control the extra term
b_{0}^{3}\partial_{3}(\sigma^{i_{4}})~(\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}+i_{4}}f)=i_{4}b_{0}^{3}(\partial_{3}\sigma)\left(\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}+1}\right)f
. Using
b_{0}^{3}|_{\Gamma}=0
, one can produce a weight function
\sigma
as in (
5.9
). Therefore

\left\|b_{0}^{3}(\partial_{3}\sigma)\left(\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}+1}f\right)\right\|_{0}\lesssim\|\partial_{3}b_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|(\sigma\partial_{3})\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}f\|_{0}\leq\|b_{0}\|_{3}\|f\|_{8,*}.

Next we analyze the commutator between
\partial_{*}^{I}=\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}
and
\nabla_{A}f
. Compared with the non-weighted case, we shall analyze an extra term
C_{\sigma}
below. In specific, one has

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}(A^{li}\partial_{l}f)

(7.1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{i_{4}}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}+i_{4}}(A^{li}\partial_{l}f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{i_{4}}\left(A^{li}\partial_{l}(\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}+i_{4}})f\right)+\underbrace{\sigma^{i_{4}}[\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}+i_{4}},A^{li}]\partial_{l}f}_{\mathring{C}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A^{li}\partial_{l}\left(\sigma^{i_{4}}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}+i_{4}}f\right)-(i_{4}\partial_{3}\sigma)\underbrace{A^{3i}\left((\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}+1}f\right)}_{C_{\sigma}}+\mathring{C}.

The term
\mathring{C}
consists of the commutators produced in the same way as the non-weighted case. It can be analyzed in the same way as in previous sections by just considering
(\sigma\partial_{3})
as a tangential derivative on the boundary. As for the extra term, we do the following computation

\displaystyle A^{3i}\left((\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}+1}f\right)

(7.2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}(A^{3i}\partial_{3}f)-\left[(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}},A^{3i}\right]\partial_{3}f

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle C^{\sigma}_{1}(f)+C^{\sigma}_{2}(f).

Note that
i_{0}+i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{4}=8-2i_{3}
. We know the leading order terms in
C^{\sigma}_{2}
are
\left((\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}A^{3i}\right)f
and
(\mathfrak{D}A^{3i})(\mathfrak{D}^{6-2i_{3}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}+1}f)
, where
\mathfrak{D}
represents any one of
(\sigma\partial_{3}),\partial_{t},\overline{\partial}{}_{1},\overline{\partial}{}_{2}
. Recall that
A^{3i}
cosists of
\overline{\partial}{}\eta\cdot\overline{\partial}{}\eta
. This shows that the highest order term in
\left((\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}A^{3i}\right)\partial_{3}f
is
(\mathfrak{D}^{8-2i_{3}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\eta)(\overline{\partial}{}\eta)f
whose
L^{2}(\Omega)
norm can be directly controlled by
\|\eta\|_{8,*}\|\overline{\partial}{}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{3}f\|_{L^{\infty}}
. As for the second term, we have

\|(\mathfrak{D}A^{3i})(\mathfrak{D}^{6-2i_{3}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}+1}f)\|_{0}\lesssim\|(\mathfrak{D}\overline{\partial}{}\eta)(\overline{\partial}{}\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|f\|_{8,*}.

Therefore,
C^{\sigma}_{2}
can be directly controlled.

The control of
C^{\sigma}_{1}
is more complicated. We should use the structure of MHD system (
1.17
) to replace one normal derivative by one tangential derivative.

\displaystyle A^{3i}\partial_{3}Q=

\displaystyle-\sum_{L=1}^{2}A^{Li}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}Q-R\partial_{t}v^{i}+J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)

(7.3)

\displaystyle A^{3i}\partial_{3}v_{i}=

\displaystyle\text{div}_{A}v-A^{1i}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}v_{i}-A^{2i}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}v_{i}=-\frac{JR^{\prime}(q)}{\rho_{0}}\partial_{t}q-\sum_{L=1}^{2}A^{Li}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i}

(7.4)

When
f=Q
, we plug (
7.3
) into
C^{\sigma}_{1}(Q)
to get

\displaystyle C^{\sigma}_{1}(Q)=

\displaystyle(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}(A^{3i}\partial_{3}Q)

(7.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{L=1}^{2}(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}(A^{Li}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}Q)

\displaystyle-(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}(R\partial_{t}v^{i})+(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta))\right)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle C_{11}^{\sigma}(Q)+C_{12}^{\sigma}(Q)+C_{13}^{\sigma}(Q).

When
f=v_{i}
, we plug (
7.4
) into
C^{\sigma}_{1}(v)
to get

\displaystyle C^{\sigma}_{1}(v)=

\displaystyle(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}(A^{3i}\partial_{3}v_{i})

(7.6)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{L=1}^{2}(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}(A^{Li}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i})-(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\left(\frac{JR^{\prime}(q)}{\rho_{0}}\partial_{t}q\right)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle C_{11}^{\sigma}(v)+C_{12}^{\sigma}(v).

The terms
C_{12}^{\sigma}(Q)
and
C_{12}^{\sigma}(v)
can be directly controlled. Note that
i_{0}+i_{1}+i_{2}+(i_{4}-1)=7-2i_{3}
, so

\displaystyle\|C_{12}^{\sigma}(Q)\|_{0}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|R\|_{7,*}\|v\|_{8,*}\lesssim\|q\|_{7,*}\|v\|_{8,*},

(7.7)

\displaystyle\|C_{12}^{\sigma}(v)\|_{0}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\rho_{0}\|_{7,*}\|q\|_{8,*}.

(7.8)

Using
b_{0}^{3}|_{\Gamma}=0
, one can produce a weight function
\sigma
as in (
5.9
) when all the derivatives fall on
J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
.

\displaystyle\|C_{13}^{\sigma}(Q)\|_{0}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\|_{0}

(7.9)

\displaystyle+\left\|\left[(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}},J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\right](J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\right\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial_{3}(J^{-1}b_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|b_{0}\|_{7,*}\|J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{8,*}.

As for
C_{11}^{\sigma}
, the highest order term can be merged into the modified Alinhac good unknowns. One has

\displaystyle C_{11}^{\sigma}(f):=

\displaystyle-\sum_{L=1}^{2}(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}(A^{Li}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}f)

(7.10)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{L=1}^{2}\left((\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}A^{Li}\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{L}f\underbrace{-\sum_{L=1}^{2}\left[(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}},A^{Li}\right]\overline{\partial}{}_{L}f}_{C_{111}^{\sigma}(f)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{L=1}^{2}A^{Lp}\left((\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\partial_{k}\eta_{p}\right)A^{ki}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}f\underbrace{-\sum_{L=1}^{2}\left(\left[(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}},A^{Lp}A^{ki}\right]\partial_{k}\eta_{p}\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{L}f}_{C_{112}^{\sigma}(f)}+C_{111}^{\sigma}(f).

Since
i_{0}+i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{4}=8-2i_{3}
, one can directly control the
L^{2}(\Omega)
-norms of
C_{111}^{\sigma}(f),~C_{112}^{\sigma}(f)
by
P(\|\eta\|_{8,*})\|f\|_{8,*}
. For the first term in the RHS of (
7.10
), one can proceed in the following ways

•

f=Q
: Since
\overline{\partial}{}_{L}Q|_{\Gamma}=0
, one can produce a weight function as in (
5.13
) and thus

\displaystyle\left\|A^{Lp}\left((\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\partial_{k}\eta_{p}\right)A^{ki}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}Q\right\|_{0}

(7.11)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{M=1}^{2}\left\|A^{Lp}\left((\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\overline{\partial}{}_{M}\eta_{p}\right)A^{Mi}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}Q\right\|_{0}+\|A^{Lp}A^{3i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{3}Q\|_{L^{\infty}}\|(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\eta_{p}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(\|\eta\|_{3})\|Q\|_{7,*}\|\eta\|_{8,*}.

•

f=v_{i}
: When
\partial_{*}^{I}
contains time derivative
(i_{0}>0)
, then it can be directly controlled due to
\partial_{t}\eta=v

\left\|A^{Lp}\left((\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\partial_{k}\eta_{p}\right)A^{ki}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i}\right\|_{0}=\left\|A^{Lp}\left((\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}-1}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\partial_{k}v_{p}\right)A^{ki}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i}\right\|_{0}\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{3})\|v\|_{5,*}\|v\|_{8,*}.

(7.12)

If
i_{0}=0
, then it can be written in the form of covariant derivative plus a controllable term.

\displaystyle-A^{Lp}\left((\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\partial_{k}\eta_{p}\right)A^{ki}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i}

(7.13)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-A^{ki}\partial_{k}\left((\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\eta_{p}A^{Lp}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i}\right)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{A^{3i}(\partial_{3}\sigma)\left((i_{4}-1)(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-2}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}+1}\eta_{p}\right)A^{Lp}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i}+\nabla_{A}^{i}(A^{Lp}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i})\left((\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\eta_{p}\right)}_{C_{113}^{\sigma}(v_{i})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nabla_{A}^{i}\left((\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\eta_{p}A^{Lp}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i}\right)+C_{113}^{\sigma}(v_{i}).

We note that the first term in
C_{113}^{\sigma}(f)
appears when
\partial_{k}~(k=3)
falls on the weight function and
i_{4}\geq 2
and can also be directly controlled by
P(\|\eta\|_{8,*})\|f\|_{8,*}
.

Next we merge the covariant derivative terms in
C_{\sigma}
into the modified Alinhac good unknowns, i.e., for
\partial_{*}^{I}=\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}
we define

\mathbf{V}^{\sigma}_{i}:=\begin{cases}\partial_{*}^{I}v_{i}-\partial_{*}^{I}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i}+(\Delta_{v}^{\sigma})_{i}~~~&i_{0}\geq 1\\
\partial_{*}^{I}v_{i}-\partial_{*}^{I}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}v_{i}+(\Delta_{v}^{\sigma})_{i}+\sum\limits_{L=1}^{2}\left((i_{4}\partial_{3}\sigma)(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\eta_{p}\right)A^{Lp}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}v_{i},~~~&i_{0}=0,\end{cases}

(7.14)

and

\mathbf{Q}^{\sigma}:=\partial_{*}^{I}Q-\partial_{*}^{I}\eta\cdot\nabla_{A}Q+\Delta_{Q}^{\sigma}.

(7.15)

Then one has

\displaystyle\partial_{*}^{I}(\nabla_{A}\cdot v)=\nabla_{A}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sigma}+C^{\sigma}(v),

(7.16)

\displaystyle\partial_{*}^{I}(\nabla_{A}Q)=\nabla_{A}\mathbf{Q}^{\sigma}+C^{\sigma}(Q),

(7.17)

with
\|C^{\sigma}(f)\|_{0}\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}(t))\|f\|_{8,*}.
Here the “extra modification terms”
\Delta_{v}^{\sigma}
and
\Delta_{Q}^{\sigma}
comes from the analysis of
\mathring{C}
in (
7.1
) whose precise expressions can be derived in the same way as Section
5

\sim
Section
6
. The term
\left((i_{4}\partial_{3}\sigma)(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}-1}\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}\eta_{p}\right)A^{Lp}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}f
comes from (
7.1
) and (
7.13
). Finally, the commutator
C^{\sigma}(f)
consists of the commutator part in
\mathring{C}
,
C_{111}^{\sigma}(f)\sim C_{113}^{\sigma}(f),C_{12}^{\sigma}(f)
and
C_{13}^{\sigma}(Q).

Recall that
\sigma|_{\Gamma}=0
and
\overline{\partial}{}Q|_{\Gamma}=0
imply
\mathbf{Q}^{\sigma}|_{\Gamma}=0
. Therefore the boundary integral
\int_{\Gamma}N_{3}\hat{A}^{3i}\mathbf{Q}^{\sigma}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}
vanishes. Hence, we can get the following estimates for
\partial_{*}^{I}:=\partial_{t}^{i_{0}}(\sigma\partial_{3})^{i_{4}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{i_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{i_{2}}\partial_{3}^{i_{3}}

\displaystyle\|\partial_{*}^{I}v\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{*}^{I}\left(J^{-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{*}^{I}q\|_{0}^{2}\bigg|_{t=T}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(7.18)

8
A priori estimates, uniqueness and continuous dependence on data

8.1
Finalizing the a priori energy estimates

Combining the
L^{2}
-energy conservation (
1.6
) with (
4.1
), (
5.1
), (
6.1
) and (
7.18
), and then choosing
\varepsilon>0
to be suitably small, we finally get the following energy inequality

\mathcal{E}(T)-\mathcal{E}(0)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t

(8.1)

under the a priori asuumptions (
1.19
)-(
1.20
). By the Gronwall-type inequality, one can find some
T_{1}>0
depending only on the initial data, such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{1}}\mathcal{E}(t)\leq P(\mathcal{E}(0)).

(8.2)

This completes the a priori estimates of (
1.17
).

8.2
Justification of the a priori assumptions

It suffices to justify the a priori assumptions (
1.19
)-(
1.20
). First, invoking
\partial_{t}J=J\text{div}_{A}v
and
J|_{t=0}=1
, we get

\|J-1\|_{7,*}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\|J\text{div}_{A}v\|_{7,*}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}})\|\partial_{t}q\|_{7,*}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}})\|q\|_{8,*}\,\mathrm{d}t.

Therefore choosing
T>0
to be sufficiently small yields (
1.19
). The Rayleigh-Taylor sign condition in
[0,T_{1}]
can be justified by proving
\partial Q/\partial N
is a Hölder-continuous function in
t
and
y
variables. In specific, from the energy estimates we know that

\frac{\partial Q}{\partial N}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{\frac{5}{2}}(\Gamma)),~\partial_{t}\left(\dfrac{\partial Q}{\partial N}\right)\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Gamma)).

By using the 2D Sobolev embedding
H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Gamma)\hookrightarrow L^{4}(\Gamma)
and Morrey’s embedding
W^{1,4}\hookrightarrow C^{0,\frac{1}{4}}
in 3D domain, we get the Hölder continuity of the Rayleigh-Taylor sign

\frac{\partial Q}{\partial N}\in W^{1,\infty}([0,T];H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Gamma))\hookrightarrow W^{1,\infty}([0,T];W^{1,4}(\Gamma))\hookrightarrow W^{1,4}([0,T]\times\Gamma)\hookrightarrow C_{t,x}^{0,\frac{1}{4}}([0,T]\times\Gamma).

Therefore, (
1.20
) holds in a positive time interval provided that
-\dfrac{\partial Q_{0}}{\partial N}\geq c_{0}>0
holds initially. Theorem
1.3
is proved.

8.3
Control of initial energy by the initial data satisfying the compatibility conditions

Finally we need to show
\mathcal{E}(0)<\infty
. Define
f_{(j)}:=\partial_{t}^{j}f|_{t=0}
to be the initial data of
\partial_{t}^{j}f
for
j\in\mathbb{N}
. We know the initial data should satisfy the following properties:

•

The compatibility conditions (
1.9
) up to 7-th order.

•

The constraints
\nabla\cdot B_{0}=0,~B_{0}\cdot n|_{\{0\}\times\partial\mathcal{D}_{0}}=0
and the Rayleigh-Taylor sign condition (
1.8
) on
\{0\}\times\partial\mathcal{D}_{0}
.

•

The norms of the initial datum of the time derivatives of
(v,b,Q)
can be controlled by the norms of initial data
(v_{0},b_{0},Q_{0})
.

We note that the compatibility conditions up to order
m
can be expressed in Lagrangian coordinates by using the formal power series solution to (
1.17
) in
t
:

\hat{v}(t,y)=\sum v_{(j)}(y)\frac{t^{j}}{j!},~\hat{b}(t,y)=\sum b_{(j)}(y)\frac{t^{j}}{j!},\hat{Q}(t,y)=\sum Q_{(j)}(y)\frac{t^{j}}{j!},

satisfying
Q_{(j)}|_{\Gamma}=0
for
j=0,1,\cdots,m
. Since we study the solutions in (anisotropic) Sobolev spaces, such compatibility conditions have to be expressed in a weak form

Q_{(j)}(y)\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega),~~0\leq j\leq m.

(8.3)

From
(v_{0},b_{0},Q_{0})\in H_{*}^{8}(\Omega)
and the system (
1.17
), one can only get
(v_{(j)},b_{(j)},Q_{(j)})\in H_{*}^{8-2j}(\Omega)
for
0\leq j\leq 4
. To guarantee
(v_{(j)},b_{(j)},Q_{(j)})\in H_{*}^{8-j}(\Omega)
and
Q_{(j)}\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)
, the initial data should be constructed in standard Sobolev space
H^{8}(\Omega)
with

\sum_{j=1}^{8}\|(v_{(j)},b_{(j)},Q_{(j)})\|_{8-j}^{2}\lesssim P(\|v_{0}\|_{8},\|b_{0}\|_{8},\|Q_{0}\|_{8}).

See the construction in
[
64
, Lemma 4.1]
.

On the one hand, by Lemma
3.2
we know
(v_{(j)},b_{(j)},Q_{(j)})\in H^{8-j}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow H_{*}^{8-j}(\Omega)
which satisfies our requirement and implies
\mathcal{E}(0)\lesssim P(\|v_{0}\|_{8},\|b_{0}\|_{8},\|Q_{0}\|_{8})
. On the other hand, if we directly construct the initial data
(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0})\in H_{*}^{8}(\Omega)
such that
(v_{(j)},b_{(j)},Q_{(j)})\in H_{*}^{8-j}(\Omega)
, then it is not clear in which sense the boundary conditions and the compatibility conditions are satisfied. For example,
Q_{(7)}\in H_{*}^{1}(\Omega)
, but the trace of such function in that space has no meaning in general. This also explains why we require
Q_{(7)}\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)
in (
8.3
). See also Secchi
[
53
, Theorem 2.1]
. Therefore, the initial data
(v_{0},b_{0},Q_{0})
should be constructed in the standard Sobolev space
H^{8}(\Omega)
.

8.4
Continuous dependence on initial data and uniqueness

Now we prove Theorem
1.4
by using a similar argument as in the proof of a priori bounds. Assume
U_{0}^{(i)}=(v_{0}^{(i)},b_{0}^{(i)},q_{0}^{(i)})\in H_{*}^{8}(\Omega)~(i=1,2)
to be two initial datum of (
1.17
) satisfying the hypothesis of Theorem
1.3
. Suppose also
U^{(i)}(t,\cdot)=(\eta^{(i)}(t,\cdot),v^{(i)}(t,\cdot),b^{(i)}(t,\cdot),q^{(i)}(t,\cdot))~(i=1,2)
to be the solutions to (
1.17
) with initial data
U_{0}^{(i)}
. Then we derive the system of
([\eta],[v],[q])
as follows, where
[f]:=f^{(1)}-f^{(2)}
for any function
f
.

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}[\eta]=[v]~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
R^{(1)}\partial_{t}[v]-{J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial\left({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta]\right)+\nabla_{A^{(1)}}[Q]+\nabla_{[A]}Q^{(2)}=f_{v}+f_{b},~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q^{(1)})\partial_{t}[q]+\text{div }_{A^{1}}[v]+\text{div }_{[A]}v^{(2)}=f_{q}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
[Q]=0~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma,\\
([\eta],[v],[b],[q])|_{t=0}=(\mathbf{0},[v_{0}],[b_{0}],[q_{0}]),\end{cases}

(8.4)

with the initial constraints (divergence-free condition for
b_{0}
,
b_{0}^{3}|_{\Gamma}=0
and the Rayleigh-Taylor sign condition) for each
i=1,2
. Here
Q^{(i)}:=q^{(i)}+\frac{1}{2}|b^{(i)}|^{2}
,
b^{(i)}={J^{(i)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(i)}\cdot\partial\eta^{(i)}
, and
\mathcal{F}(q^{(i)}):=\log R^{(i)}(q^{(i)})
. The source terms
f_{v,b}
and
f_{q}
are defined by

\displaystyle f_{v}:=

\displaystyle-[R]\partial_{t}v^{(2)}+[b_{0}]\cdot\partial({J^{(2)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(2)}\cdot\partial\eta^{(2)}),~f_{b}:=b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial([J^{-1}b_{0}]\cdot\partial\eta^{(2)}),

(8.5)

\displaystyle f_{q}:=

\displaystyle-(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q^{(1)})-\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q^{(2)}))\partial_{t}q^{(2)}.

(8.6)

Note that system (
8.4
) has the same structure as (
1.17
) on the left side of each equation. And it is not difficult to see that the
\|\cdot\|_{6,*}
norm of both source terms can be directly controlled, because each solution
U^{(i)}
are bounded in
\|\cdot\|_{8,*}
norm. In particular, the second term and the fourth term in
f_{b,v}
can be controlled by writing the
[\cdot]
terms back to the form
f^{(1)}-f^{(2)}
and using the bounds for each
U^{(i)}
. Therefore, the estimates for
([\eta],[v],[b],[q])
in
\|\cdot\|_{6,*}
norm should follow in a similar way as in the proof of Theorem
1.3
. It is even easier because we no longer need to design the “modified good unknowns” when taking
\partial_{*}^{I}
with
\langle I\rangle=6
. Indeed, given the derivative
\partial_{*}^{I}
with
\langle I\rangle=6
, we define
\mathbf{F}^{(i)}:=\partial_{*}^{I}f^{(i)}-\partial_{*}^{I}\eta^{(i)}\cdot\nabla_{A^{(i)}}f^{(i)},~i=1,2,
to be the Alinhac good unknowns for
f^{(i)}
with respect to
\partial_{*}^{I}
. Then define
[\mathbf{F}]:=\mathbf{F}^{(1)}-\mathbf{F}^{(2)}
and we have

\displaystyle[\mathbf{F}]=

\displaystyle~\partial_{*}^{I}[f]-\partial_{*}^{I}\eta^{(1)}\cdot\nabla_{A^{(1)}}[f]-\partial_{*}^{I}\eta^{(1)}\cdot\nabla_{[A]}f^{(2)}-\partial_{*}^{I}[\eta]\cdot\nabla_{A^{(2)}}f^{(2)},

(8.7)

\displaystyle\partial_{*}^{I}(\nabla_{A^{(1)}}[f]+\nabla_{[A]}f^{(2)})=

\displaystyle~\nabla_{A^{(1)}}[\mathbf{F}]+C^{(1)}([f]),

(8.8)

\displaystyle\|[\mathbf{F}]-\partial_{*}^{I}[f]\|_{0}+

\displaystyle~\|\partial_{t}([\mathbf{F}]-\partial_{*}^{I}[f])\|_{0}+\|C^{(1)}([f])\|_{0}\leq P(\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(t),\mathcal{E}^{(2)}(t))[\mathcal{E}](t).

(8.9)

We also obtain the evolution equation for the good unknown
[\mathbf{V}]

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{(1)}\partial_{t}[\mathbf{V}]-b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial\partial_{*}^{I}\left({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta]\right)+\nabla_{A^{(1)}}[\mathbf{Q}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\partial_{*}^{I}f_{b}+\underbrace{\partial_{*}^{I}f_{v}+C^{(1)}([Q])-J^{(1)}[\partial_{*}^{I},R^{(1)}]\partial_{t}[v]-J^{(1)}[\partial_{*}^{I},{J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial]\left({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta]\right)}_{=:\mathbf{G}},

(8.10)

where
\mathbf{G}
satisfies
\|\mathbf{G}\|_{0}\leq P(\mathcal{E}(t))[\mathcal{E}](t)
.

Multiplying
[\mathbf{V}]
in (
8.4
) and integrating by part, we still get the following terms as in previous sections

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\rho_{0}^{(1)}|[\mathbf{V}]|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}N_{3}[\mathbf{Q}]J^{(1)}A^{(1)3i}[\mathbf{V}]_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}+\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{G}\cdot[\mathbf{V}]\,\mathrm{d}y+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{*}^{I}f_{b}\cdot[\mathbf{V}]\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}J^{(1)}[\mathbf{Q}]\nabla_{A^{(1)}}\cdot[\mathbf{V}]\,\mathrm{d}y-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{*}^{I}\left({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta]\right)(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\mathbf{V}]\,\mathrm{d}y,

(8.11)

where the second term on the right side can be directly bounded. For the third term, we can integrate
b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial
by parts to avoid more than 6 derivatives falling on
[J^{-1}b_{0}]
. When
b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial
falls on
\partial_{*}^{I}[v]
, we write
[v]=\partial_{t}\eta
and integrate by parts in
\partial_{t}
to control this term.

Since most of the steps are identical to the previous sections, we no longer repeat all those details. Below, we show the details of some key steps that are slightly different from the previous sections, and we only take
\overline{\partial}{}^{6}
-estimates and
\partial_{3}^{3}
estimates for examples.

Interior cancellation structure in Section
2.3
.

We take
\partial_{*}^{I}=\overline{\partial}{}^{6}
for example. Plugging the expression of
[\mathbf{V}]
into the last term in (
8.4
), we get

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\left({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta]\right)(b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial)[\mathbf{V}]\,\mathrm{d}y

(8.12)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}J^{(1)}\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\left({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta]\right)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}J^{(1)}\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\left({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta]\right)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}J^{(1)}\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\left({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta]\right)\cdot\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\partial_{t},J^{(1)}\right]({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta])\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\left({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta]\right)\cdot\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{6},b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial\right]([v])\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\left({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta]\right)(b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial)\left(\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\eta^{(1)}\cdot\nabla_{A^{(1)}}[v]-\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\eta^{(1)}\cdot\nabla_{[A]}v^{(2)}-\overline{\partial}{}^{6}[\eta]\cdot\nabla_{A^{(2)}}v^{(2)}\right)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}J^{(1)}\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\left({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta]\right)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y+P(\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(t),\mathcal{E}^{(2)}(t))[\mathcal{E}](t),

where we note that the third term on the right side contains the analogue of
K_{11}
term defined in Section
2.3
, that is,
\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\partial_{t}
may fall on
J^{(1)}
. Here we already know
\|J^{(1)}(t,\cdot)\|_{8,*}\leq P(\mathcal{E}(t))
and thus this term can be directly controlled.

Similarly, the fourth term in (
8.4
) will produce the energy term of
[q]
as stated in Section
2.3
plus the term

\int_{\Omega}J^{(1)}\overline{\partial}{}^{6}({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta])({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta])\overline{\partial}{}^{6}(\nabla_{A^{(1)}}\cdot[v])\,\mathrm{d}y,

which is also directly controlled by
P(\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(t),\mathcal{E}^{(2)}(t))[\mathcal{E}](t)
due to
\|A^{(1)}(t,\cdot)\|_{6,*}\lesssim\|\eta(t,\cdot)\|_{8,*}^{2}\leq P(\mathcal{E}(t))
.

Boundary energy in
\overline{\partial}{}^{6}
-estimates.

We plug
[Q]|_{\Gamma}=0
and
Q^{(i)}=0
into the boundary integral to get

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}[\mathbf{Q}]N_{3}A^{(1)3i}[\mathbf{V}]_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}N_{3}\partial_{3}Q^{(1)}J^{(1)}A^{(1)3j}\overline{\partial}{}^{6}[\eta]_{j}A^{(1)3i}[\mathbf{V}]_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

(8.13)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Gamma}N_{3}\partial_{3}Q^{(1)}(\overline{\partial}{}^{6}[\eta]_{j}A^{(1)3j}+\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\eta_{j}^{(2)}[A]^{3j})A^{(1)3i}[\mathbf{V}]_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Gamma}(-N_{3}\partial_{3}Q^{(1)})|A^{(1)3i}\overline{\partial}{}^{6}[\eta]_{i}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(-N_{3}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}Q^{(1)})|A^{(1)3i}\overline{\partial}{}^{6}[\eta]_{i}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Gamma}N_{3}\partial_{3}Q^{(1)}A^{(1)3j}\overline{\partial}{}^{6}[\eta]_{j}A^{(1)3i}(\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\eta_{r}[A]^{kr}\partial_{k}v_{i}^{(1)}-\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\eta_{r}^{(2)}A^{(2)kr}\partial_{k}[v]_{i})\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Gamma}N_{3}\partial_{3}Q^{(1)}(\overline{\partial}{}^{6}[\eta]_{j}A^{(1)3j}+\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\eta_{j}^{(2)}[A]^{3j})A^{(1)3i}[\mathbf{V}]_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\frac{c_{0}}{4}\left|A^{(1)3i}\overline{\partial}{}^{6}[\eta]_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}+P(\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(0),\mathcal{E}^{(2)}(0))[\mathcal{E}](t).

where we use the Rayleigh-Taylor sign condition for
Q^{(1)}
in the first term. The other terms can be controlled after integrating by parts in
\partial_{t}
or in
\overline{\partial}{}
and using the trace lemma:
|[v]|_{5}\lesssim\|[v]\|_{6,*}
and
|\eta^{(i)}|_{7}\lesssim\|\eta^{(i)}\|_{8,*}
.

Boundary terms in
\partial_{3}^{3}
-estimates.

The boundary integral now reads

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}N_{3}J^{(1)}[\mathbf{Q}][\mathbf{V}]_{i}A^{(1)3i}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime},

(8.14)

where

[\mathbf{F}]=\partial_{3}^{3}[f]-\partial_{3}^{3}\eta^{(1)}\cdot\nabla_{A^{(1)}}[f]-\partial_{3}^{3}\eta^{(1)}\cdot\nabla_{[A]}f^{(2)}-\partial_{3}^{3}[\eta]\cdot\nabla_{A^{(2)}}f^{(2)}.

What we need to do is again to reduce one
\partial_{3}
falling on
[Q],[v]_{i}
to a tangential derivative by repeatedly invoking system (
8.4
). Here we only list the identities analogous to those in Section
4.3
. We first have

\displaystyle\partial_{3}^{3}\eta^{(1)}_{j}A^{(1)3j}=\partial_{3}^{2}(\underbrace{\partial_{3}\eta^{(1)}_{j}A^{(1)3j}}_{=1})-[\partial_{3}^{2},A^{(1)3j}]\partial_{3}\eta_{(j)}.

(8.15)

Then the third component of the second equation in (
8.4
) gives

\displaystyle J^{(1)}A^{(1)3k}\partial_{3}^{3}[Q]=

\displaystyle\partial_{3}^{2}(\nabla_{\hat{A}^{(1)}}^{k}[Q])-[\partial_{3}^{2},A^{(1)3k}]\partial_{3}[Q]

(8.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{3}^{2}\left(-\rho_{0}^{(1)}\partial_{t}[v]^{k}+b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial({J^{(1)}}^{-1}b_{0}^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta]^{k})+f_{v}^{k}+f_{b}^{k}-\nabla_{[A]}^{k}Q^{(2)}\right)

\displaystyle-\sum_{L=1}^{2}\partial_{3}^{2}(A^{(1)Li}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}Q)-[\partial_{3}^{2},A^{(1)3k}]\partial_{3}[Q],

and thus the top-order derivative on
[Q],[v]
becomes
\partial_{3}^{2}\mathfrak{D}
for some tangential derivative
\mathfrak{D}
. Note also that
\partial_{3}^{2}[A]^{mk}\partial_{m}Q^{(2)}=\partial_{3}^{2}[A]^{3k}\partial_{3}Q^{(2)}
due to
Q^{(2)}|_{\Gamma}=0
and
A^{3l}
only contains tangential derivative
\overline{\partial}{}\eta
. The term
\partial_{3}^{2}A^{(1)Li}
can be directly controlled by
\|\eta^{(1)}\|_{4}^{2}\leq\mathcal{E}(t)
, so we no longer needs the subtle cancellation structures introduced in section
4.3
. Similarly, using the third equation of
8.4
, we get

\displaystyle A^{(1)3i}\partial_{3}^{3}[v]_{i}=

\displaystyle\partial_{3}^{2}(\text{div }_{A^{(1)}}[v])-[\partial_{3}^{2},A^{(1)3i}]\partial_{3}[v]_{i}

(8.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{3}^{2}\left(-\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q^{(1)})\partial_{t}[q]+f_{q}-\text{div }_{[A]}v^{(2)}\right)-\sum_{L=1}^{2}\partial_{3}^{2}(A^{(1)Li}\overline{\partial}{}_{L}[v]_{i})-[\partial_{3}^{2},A^{(1)3i}]\partial_{3}[v]_{i},

where the term
-\partial_{3}^{2}\text{div }_{[A]}v^{(2)}
may have a term in which
\partial_{3}^{2}
falls on
\partial_{3}[\eta]
, that is,
A^{(2)lr}\partial_{3}^{3}[\eta]_{r}A^{(1)3i}\partial_{l}v_{i}^{(2)}=A^{(1)3i}\partial_{3}^{3}[\eta]\cdot\nabla_{A^{(2)}}v^{(2)}
which cancels with the last term in
A^{(1)3i}[\mathbf{V}]_{i}
.

After these reductions, the top-order terms on the boundary becomes the following form, which is controlled analogously to (
2.17
). For
\langle I\rangle=6
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}N_{3}(\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}\mathfrak{D}[f])(\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}\mathfrak{D}[g])h\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

(8.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{3}\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}\mathfrak{D}[f])(\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}\mathfrak{D}[g])h\,\mathrm{d}y+\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}\mathfrak{D}[f])(\partial_{3}\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}\mathfrak{D}[g])h\,\mathrm{d}y+\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}\mathfrak{D}[f])(\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}\mathfrak{D}[g])\partial_{3}h\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\overset{\mathfrak{D}}{=}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{3}\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}[f])(\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}\mathfrak{D}^{2}[g])h\,\mathrm{d}y+\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{3}\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}[f])(\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}\mathfrak{D}[g])\mathfrak{D}h\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}\mathfrak{D}^{2}[f])(\partial_{3}\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}[g])h\,\mathrm{d}y+\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}\mathfrak{D}[f])(\partial_{3}\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}[g])\mathfrak{D}h\,\mathrm{d}y+\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}\mathfrak{D}[f])(\partial_{*}^{I-e_{3}}\mathfrak{D}[g])\partial_{3}h\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|[f]\|_{6,*}\|[g]\|_{6,*}\|h\|_{3},

where the terms in
h
only have at most one derivative on each variable.

Define the energy functional for (
8.4
)

[\mathcal{E}](t):=\|[\eta](t,\cdot)\|_{6,*}^{2}+\|[v](t,\cdot)\|_{6,*}^{2}+\|(J^{-1}b_{0})^{(1)}\cdot\partial[\eta](t,\cdot)\|_{6,*}^{2}+\|[q](t,\cdot)\|_{6,*}^{2}+\sum_{\langle I\rangle=6}\left|A^{(1)3i}\partial_{*}^{I}[\eta]_{i}\right|_{0}^{2}.

(8.19)

We can finally get the estimates for (
8.4
)

[\mathcal{E}](t)\leq[\mathcal{E}](0)+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(t),\mathcal{E}^{(2)}(t))[\mathcal{E}](t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

(8.20)

and thus by Grönwall’s inequality, there exists some
T_{2}\in[0,T_{1}]
(
T_{1}>0
is the time for the a priori bounds obtained in Theorem
1.3
) depending only on the initial data and
c_{0}
such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{2}}\mathcal{E}(t)\leq P(\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(0),\mathcal{E}^{(2)}(0))[\mathcal{E}](0)\leq P(\|[v_{0}],[b_{0}],[Q_{0}]\|_{6})P(\||v_{0}^{(1)},v_{0}^{(2)}\|_{8},\|b_{0}^{(1)},b_{0}^{(2)}\|_{8},\|Q_{0}^{(1)},Q_{0}^{(2)}\|_{8}).

Note that the energy inequality is linear in
[\mathcal{E}](t)
because (
8.4
) is a
linear
system of
[v],[b],[Q]
. In particular, if the two given initial datum are equal, we must have
[\mathcal{E}](0)=0
and thus
[\mathcal{E}](t)=0
in
[0,T_{2}]
. This proves the uniqueness and continuous dependence on initial data provided the local-in-time solution exists.

9
On the local existence of solutions

9.1
Local existence theorem for smooth data satisfying compatibility conditions up to infinite order

As stated in Section
2.6
, we need to prove Theorem
1.2
, that is, a local existence theorem from
C^{\infty}
data to
C^{\infty}
solution. Following the Nash-Moser iteration scheme presented in Alinhac-Gérard
[
3
]
and Secchi
[
54
]
(also adopted in
[
38
,
40
,
64
,
65
]
), to solve a nonlinear system
L(U)=f
, we start with an approximate solution
U^{a}
and then
U=U^{a}+V
is a solution to
L(U)=f
if we can prove
V
solves
\mathbf{L}(V):=L(U^{a}+V)-L(U^{a})=f^{a}
where
f^{a}:=-L(U^{a})
with
V|_{t=0}=0
.

Remark

(Existence of smooth approximate solution)
.

In
[
64
, Lemma 4.1]
, an approximate solution was constructed in Sobolev space. Here we can follow Lindblad
[
40
, Lemma 16.3]
to construct a smooth approximate solution by introducing the power series
Q^{a}(t,y):=\sum_{k}\chi(t/\varepsilon_{k})Q_{(k)}(y)t^{k}/k!
where
Q_{(k)}:=\partial_{t}^{k}Q|_{t=0}\in C^{\infty}(\bar{\Omega})\cap H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)
are defined in Appendix
A.3
,
\chi(\cdot)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is a smooth cut-off function which equals to 1 in
[-1,1]
and vanishes outside
[-2,2]
, and
\varepsilon_{k}>0
are chosen suitably small such that the series converges in
H^{N}(\Omega)
for any
N
. Then solve
v^{a},\eta^{a}
from the MHD system (
1.17
).

To solve the increment
V
, we start with
V_{0}=0
and inductively define
V_{n+1}=V_{n}+\delta V_{n}
where
\delta V_{n}
is the solution to the linearized problem
\mathbf{L}^{\prime}(U^{a}+S_{\theta_{n}}V_{n})(\delta V_{n})=f_{n}
with
S_{\theta_{n}}
being the smoothing operator,
